People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are these feelings making it hard for you to function at work or take care of things at home?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I’m still showing up to work and I’m doing my best, so it’s not like I’ve completely fallen apart or anything. But my supervisor did mention my charting hasn’t been finished the way it usually is, which was embarrassing.\n\nAnd at home… I’m taking care of the basics, but I’ve been kind of pulling back from friends and from the things I normally do to keep myself steady. I don’t know if that counts as “hard to function,” but it feels harder than usual.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I still could do those things, I guess, but I just haven’t really wanted to.\n\nAfter my shifts I used to paint, or go to yoga or salsa, just to decompress. Lately I mostly just go home and sit on the couch… and I ignore texts sometimes because none of it feels worth the effort. That sounds bad, doesn’t it? I’m trying not to make it a bigger deal than it is, but it’s definitely different for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it hopeless exactly… maybe that sounds too strong. But for the past few weeks there’s been this heavy, sad pressure on me almost all day.\n\nEven when I’m at work and I’m smiling at patients, inside I feel like I’m barely holding myself together. I’m sorry, I don’t know if that answers the question.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that you’d be better off dead?\n\nPatient: Um… yes, kind of. I hate saying that, because I don’t want you to think I’m trying to hurt myself or anything.\n\nBut I have had thoughts like… I wish I could just not wake up. I mean, I worry, but I don’t know if it’s more than a normal amount, if that makes sense.\n\nI haven’t really thought of it as anxiety, exactly. But for the past few weeks there’s been this heavy, sad pressure on me almost all day.\n\nEven when I’m at work and I’m smiling at patients, inside I feel like I’m barely holding myself together. I’m sorry, I don’t know if that answers the question.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that you’d be better off dead?\n\nPatient: Um… yes, kind of. I hate saying that, because I don’t want you to think I’m trying to hurt myself or anything.\n\nBut I have had thoughts like… I wish I could just not wake up. That’s actually what scared me enough to bring it up at my prenatal appointment. I don’t have a plan to hurt myself, but the thoughts being there at all really scared me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure. I mean, I worry, but I don’t know if it’s more than a normal amount, if that makes sense.\n\nI haven’t really thought of it as anxiety, exactly. It’s more just… I feel off and tense sometimes, but maybe that’s just me being overwhelmed. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem mainly like a reaction to finding out you’re pregnant?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, it does feel tied to that. It started maybe shortly after I found out I was pregnant, and it’s been about three weeks now.\n\nI don’t want to blame the pregnancy or make it sound dramatic, but the timing does line up.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than you would expect as a reaction to finding out you’re pregnant?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think maybe they are stronger than I expected. I mean, I know finding out you’re pregnant is a lot, and I don’t want to overreact or sound ungrateful, but this feels… heavier than just being surprised or stressed.\n\nFor the past few weeks it’s been like this sad pressure sitting on me most of the day. Even at work, I can smile and talk to patients like I’m okay, but inside I feel like I’m barely keeping it together. Is that… is that more than what you’d normally expect?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this sad most days for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It’s been most days for the past few weeks.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I’m still going to work and doing what I’m supposed to do, but there’s this heavy, sad pressure that’s there almost all day. Even when I’m smiling with patients, inside I feel like I’m barely holding myself together.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in your appetite?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I mean… I know I should be eating regularly, especially now, and I’m trying not to be careless about it. But most days food just doesn’t sound good.\n\nSometimes I’ll get halfway through a clinic shift and realize I’ve only had crackers or coffee all day. I know that’s not ideal. I’m not trying to do that on purpose, it just… happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I’m… not totally sure. I mean, I don’t think it’s been anything dramatic, or at least I haven’t been tracking it closely.\n\nSome nights I feel like I’m not resting well, but I also don’t want to blame everything on sleep, if that makes sense. What would count as “trouble sleeping” to you?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling slowed down, like your body or thoughts are moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t function, because I am getting through my shifts, but everything feels… slowed down.\n\nLike I’m moving through syrup, almost. Even simple things at the clinic — gathering wound supplies, finishing charting — they’re taking me longer than they should. I keep noticing it and feeling embarrassed, because it’s not usually like me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or excessively guilty lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I’m not sure I’d call it worthless. That feels a little strong.\n\nI do second-guess myself a lot lately, and I feel bad when I’m not keeping up the way I think I should. But I also know everyone has off periods, so I don’t want to overstate it. Is that the kind of thing you mean, or do you mean more like… really believing I’m a bad person?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I have. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m unsafe or anything, but my brain just feels foggy.\n\nAt work I’ll catch myself rereading medication orders or wound-care notes more than once, just to make sure I’m not missing something. And decisions that I’d normally make kind of automatically… I freeze a little and second-guess myself.\n\nIs that what you mean by trouble concentrating?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

It started maybe shortly after I found out I was pregnant, and it’s been about three weeks now.\n\nI don’t want to blame the pregnancy or make it sound dramatic, but the timing does line up.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than you would expect as a reaction to finding out you’re pregnant?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think maybe they are stronger than I expected. I mean, I know finding out you’re pregnant is a lot, and I don’t want to overreact or sound ungrateful, but this feels… heavier than just being surprised or stressed.\n\nFor the past few weeks it’s been like this sad pressure sitting on me most of the day. Even at work, I can smile and talk to patients like I’m okay, but inside I feel like I’m barely keeping it together. Is that… is that more than what you’d normally expect?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this sad most days for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It’s been most days for the past few weeks.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I’m still going to work and doing what I’m supposed to do, but there’s this heavy, sad pressure that’s there almost all day. Even when I’m smiling with patients, inside I feel like I’m barely holding myself together.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in your appetite?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I mean… I know I should be eating regularly, especially now, and I’m trying not to be careless about it. But most days food just doesn’t sound good.\n\nSometimes I’ll get halfway through a clinic shift and realize I’ve only had crackers or coffee all day. I know that’s not ideal. I’m not trying to do that on purpose, it just… happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I’m… not totally sure. I mean, I don’t think it’s been anything dramatic, or at least I haven’t been tracking it closely.\n\nSome nights I feel like I’m not resting well, but I also don’t want to blame everything on sleep, if that makes sense. What would count as “trouble sleeping” to you?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling slowed down, like your body or thoughts are moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t function, because I am getting through my shifts, but everything feels… slowed down.\n\nLike I’m moving through syrup, almost. Even simple things at the clinic — gathering wound supplies, finishing charting — they’re taking me longer than they should. I keep noticing it and feeling embarrassed, because it’s not usually like me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or excessively guilty lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I’m not sure I’d call it worthless. That feels a little strong.\n\nI do second-guess myself a lot lately, and I feel bad when I’m not keeping up the way I think I should. But I also know everyone has off periods, so I don’t want to overstate it. Is that the kind of thing you mean, or do you mean more like… really believing I’m a bad person?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I have. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m unsafe or anything, but my brain just feels foggy.\n\nAt work I’ll catch myself rereading medication orders or wound-care notes more than once, just to make sure I’m not missing something. And decisions that I’d normally make kind of automatically… I freeze a little and second-guess myself.\n\nIs that what you mean by trouble concentrating?\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings been causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess they have. I mean, I’m still showing up to work and doing my shifts, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart.\n\nBut my supervisor has noticed some of my charting hasn’t been finished, which is… really embarrassing for me. And outside of work I’ve been kind of pulling away from friends, not doing the things that usually help me feel more steady.\n\nSo yes, it is distressing. I just keep hoping it’s not as obvious as it feels.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Even at work, I can smile and talk to patients like I’m okay, but inside I feel like I’m barely keeping it together. Is that… is that more than what you’d normally expect?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this sad most days for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It’s been most days for the past few weeks.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I’m still going to work and doing what I’m supposed to do, but there’s this heavy, sad pressure that’s there almost all day. Even when I’m smiling with patients, inside I feel like I’m barely holding myself together.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in your appetite?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I mean… I know I should be eating regularly, especially now, and I’m trying not to be careless about it. But most days food just doesn’t sound good.\n\nSometimes I’ll get halfway through a clinic shift and realize I’ve only had crackers or coffee all day. I know that’s not ideal. I’m not trying to do that on purpose, it just… happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I’m… not totally sure. I mean, I don’t think it’s been anything dramatic, or at least I haven’t been tracking it closely.\n\nSome nights I feel like I’m not resting well, but I also don’t want to blame everything on sleep, if that makes sense. What would count as “trouble sleeping” to you?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling slowed down, like your body or thoughts are moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t function, because I am getting through my shifts, but everything feels… slowed down.\n\nLike I’m moving through syrup, almost. Even simple things at the clinic — gathering wound supplies, finishing charting — they’re taking me longer than they should. I keep noticing it and feeling embarrassed, because it’s not usually like me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or excessively guilty lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I’m not sure I’d call it worthless. That feels a little strong.\n\nI do second-guess myself a lot lately, and I feel bad when I’m not keeping up the way I think I should. But I also know everyone has off periods, so I don’t want to overstate it. Is that the kind of thing you mean, or do you mean more like… really believing I’m a bad person?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I have. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m unsafe or anything, but my brain just feels foggy.\n\nAt work I’ll catch myself rereading medication orders or wound-care notes more than once, just to make sure I’m not missing something. And decisions that I’d normally make kind of automatically… I freeze a little and second-guess myself.\n\nIs that what you mean by trouble concentrating?\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings been causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess they have. I mean, I’m still showing up to work and doing my shifts, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart.\n\nBut my supervisor has noticed some of my charting hasn’t been finished, which is… really embarrassing for me. And outside of work I’ve been kind of pulling away from friends, not doing the things that usually help me feel more steady.\n\nSo yes, it is distressing. I just keep hoping it’s not as obvious as it feels.\n\nDoctor: Over the past few weeks, have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean… I’ve had some nights that weren’t great, but I don’t know if it’s enough to call it “trouble sleeping.”\n\nI haven’t really been tracking it or anything. I guess I can tell you what’s been happening if that helps—what counts as trouble sleeping, exactly?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem mainly like a reaction to finding out you’re pregnant?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, it does feel tied to that. It started maybe shortly after I found out I was pregnant, and it’s been about three weeks now.\n\nI don’t want to blame the pregnancy or make it sound dramatic, but the timing does line up.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than you would expect as a reaction to finding out you’re pregnant?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think maybe they are stronger than I expected. I mean, I know finding out you’re pregnant is a lot, and I don’t want to overreact or sound ungrateful, but this feels… heavier than just being surprised or stressed.\n\nFor the past few weeks it’s been like this sad pressure sitting on me most of the day. Even at work, I can smile and talk to patients like I’m okay, but inside I feel like I’m barely keeping it together. Is that… is that more than what you’d normally expect?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this sad most days for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It’s been most days for the past few weeks.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I’m still going to work and doing what I’m supposed to do, but there’s this heavy, sad pressure that’s there almost all day. Even when I’m smiling with patients, inside I feel like I’m barely holding myself together.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in your appetite?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I mean… I know I should be eating regularly, especially now, and I’m trying not to be careless about it. But most days food just doesn’t sound good.\n\nSometimes I’ll get halfway through a clinic shift and realize I’ve only had crackers or coffee all day. I know that’s not ideal. I’m not trying to do that on purpose, it just… happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I’m… not totally sure. I mean, I don’t think it’s been anything dramatic, or at least I haven’t been tracking it closely.\n\nSome nights I feel like I’m not resting well, but I also don’t want to blame everything on sleep, if that makes sense. What would count as “trouble sleeping” to you?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling slowed down, like your body or thoughts are moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t function, because I am getting through my shifts, but everything feels… slowed down.\n\nLike I’m moving through syrup, almost. Even simple things at the clinic — gathering wound supplies, finishing charting — they’re taking me longer than they should. I keep noticing it and feeling embarrassed, because it’s not usually like me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or excessively guilty lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I’m not sure I’d call it worthless. That feels a little strong.\n\nI do second-guess myself a lot lately, and I feel bad when I’m not keeping up the way I think I should. But I also know everyone has off periods, so I don’t want to overstate it. Is that the kind of thing you mean, or do you mean more like… really believing I’m a bad person?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I have. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m unsafe or anything, but my brain just feels foggy.\n\nAt work I’ll catch myself rereading medication orders or wound-care notes more than once, just to make sure I’m not missing something. And decisions that I’d normally make kind of automatically… I freeze a little and second-guess myself.\n\nIs that what you mean by trouble concentrating?\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings been causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess they have. I mean, I’m still showing up to work and doing my shifts, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart.\n\nBut my supervisor has noticed some of my charting hasn’t been finished, which is… really embarrassing for me. And outside of work I’ve been kind of pulling away from friends, not doing the things that usually help me feel more steady.\n\nSo yes, it is distressing. I just keep hoping it’s not as obvious as it feels.\n\nDoctor: Over the past few weeks, have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean… I’ve had some nights that weren’t great, but I don’t know if it’s enough to call it “trouble sleeping.”\n\nI haven’t really been tracking it or anything. I guess I can tell you what’s been happening if that helps—what counts as trouble sleeping, exactly?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt abnormally energized or unusually up, with much less need for sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like that.\n\nI mean, I’ve had times where I pushed through being tired, like with work or just trying to get things done, but I wouldn’t say I felt unusually energized or “up.” And I definitely don’t remember needing much less sleep and feeling fine.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don’t know if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Did something stressful happen in your life around the time these feelings started?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess the main thing was that I found out I’m pregnant. It’s been about three weeks since then, and that’s around when this started. I feel bad saying that, because it’s supposed to be good news, right? But… yeah, the timing lines up.\n\nDoctor: Before this pregnancy, have you ever had another time in your life when you felt depressed like this for days or weeks?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had stressful times, like anyone, and I’ve been sad before, but not really like this where it kind of hangs around day after day. At least not that I can remember. Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are these feelings making it hard for you to function at work or take care of things at home?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I’m still showing up to work and I’m doing my best, so it’s not like I’ve completely fallen apart or anything. But my supervisor did mention my charting hasn’t been finished the way it usually is, which was embarrassing.\n\nAnd at home… I’m taking care of the basics, but I’ve been kind of pulling back from friends and from the things I normally do to keep myself steady. I don’t know if that counts as “hard to function,” but it feels harder than usual.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I still could do those things, I guess, but I just haven’t really wanted to.\n\nAfter my shifts I used to paint, or go to yoga or salsa, just to decompress. Lately I mostly just go home and sit on the couch… and I ignore texts sometimes because none of it feels worth the effort. That sounds bad, doesn’t it? I’m trying not to make it a bigger deal than it is, but it’s definitely different for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it hopeless exactly… maybe that sounds too strong. But for the past few weeks there’s been this heavy, sad pressure on me almost all day.\n\nEven when I’m at work and I’m smiling at patients, inside I feel like I’m barely holding myself together. I’m sorry, I don’t know if that answers the question.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that you’d be better off dead?\n\nPatient: Um… yes, kind of. I hate saying that, because I don’t want you to think I’m trying to hurt myself or anything.\n\nBut I have had thoughts like… I wish I could just not wake up. That’s actually what scared me enough to bring it up at my prenatal appointment. I don’t have a plan to hurt myself, but the thoughts being there at all really scared me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure. I mean, I worry, but I don’t know if it’s more than a normal amount, if that makes sense.\n\nI haven’t really thought of it as anxiety, exactly. It’s more just… I feel off and tense sometimes, but maybe that’s just me being overwhelmed. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem mainly like a reaction to finding out you’re pregnant?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, it does feel tied to that. It started maybe shortly after I found out I was pregnant, and it’s been about three weeks now.\n\nI don’t want to blame the pregnancy or make it sound dramatic, but the timing does line up.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than you would expect as a reaction to finding out you’re pregnant?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think maybe they are stronger than I expected. I mean, I know finding out you’re pregnant is a lot, and I don’t want to overreact or sound ungrateful, but this feels… heavier than just being surprised or stressed.\n\nFor the past few weeks it’s been like this sad pressure sitting on me most of the day. Even at work, I can smile and talk to patients like I’m okay, but inside I feel like I’m barely keeping it together. Is that… is that more than what you’d normally expect?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this sad most days for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It’s been most days for the past few weeks.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I’m still going to work and doing what I’m supposed to do, but there’s this heavy, sad pressure that’s there almost all day. Even when I’m smiling with patients, inside I feel like I’m barely holding myself together.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in your appetite?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I mean… I know I should be eating regularly, especially now, and I’m trying not to be careless about it. But most days food just doesn’t sound good.\n\nSometimes I’ll get halfway through a clinic shift and realize I’ve only had crackers or coffee all day. I know that’s not ideal. I’m not trying to do that on purpose, it just… happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I’m… not totally sure. I mean, I don’t think it’s been anything dramatic, or at least I haven’t been tracking it closely.\n\nSome nights I feel like I’m not resting well, but I also don’t want to blame everything on sleep, if that makes sense. What would count as “trouble sleeping” to you?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling slowed down, like your body or thoughts are moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t function, because I am getting through my shifts, but everything feels… slowed down.\n\nLike I’m moving through syrup, almost. Even simple things at the clinic — gathering wound supplies, finishing charting — they’re taking me longer than they should. I keep noticing it and feeling embarrassed, because it’s not usually like me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or excessively guilty lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I’m not sure I’d call it worthless. That feels a little strong.\n\nI do second-guess myself a lot lately, and I feel bad when I’m not keeping up the way I think I should. But I also know everyone has off periods, so I don’t want to overstate it. Is that the kind of thing you mean, or do you mean more like… really believing I’m a bad person?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I have. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m unsafe or anything, but my brain just feels foggy.\n\nAt work I’ll catch myself rereading medication orders or wound-care notes more than once, just to make sure I’m not missing something. And decisions that I’d normally make kind of automatically… I freeze a little and second-guess myself.\n\nIs that what you mean by trouble concentrating?\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings been causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess they have. I mean, I’m still showing up to work and doing my shifts, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart.\n\nBut my supervisor has noticed some of my charting hasn’t been finished, which is… really embarrassing for me. And outside of work I’ve been kind of pulling away from friends, not doing the things that usually help me feel more steady.\n\nSo yes, it is distressing. I just keep hoping it’s not as obvious as it feels.\n\nDoctor: Over the past few weeks, have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean… I’ve had some nights that weren’t great, but I don’t know if it’s enough to call it “trouble sleeping.”\n\nI haven’t really been tracking it or anything. I guess I can tell you what’s been happening if that helps—what counts as trouble sleeping, exactly?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt abnormally energized or unusually up, with much less need for sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like that.\n\nI mean, I’ve had times where I pushed through being tired, like with work or just trying to get things done, but I wouldn’t say I felt unusually energized or “up.” And I definitely don’t remember needing much less sleep and feeling fine.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings have significantly interfered with how you function at work or in your daily life?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, I think so. I’m still getting to work and doing my shifts, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nBut my supervisor has noticed some of my charting hasn’t been finished, and that’s not really like me. And outside of work I’ve been pulling back from friends and not doing the things that usually keep me steady.\n\nSo… yeah, I guess it has interfered. Does that make sense?"
